# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review — round 7

Reviewed HEAD **`9bd7cb4b5d9270bbe99cb5dfec3993502f6b5377`**, 2026-11-03 KST. **Approval remains blocked.** The exact named m1–m6 originator-leak reproductions are repaired, but the new whole-name shortcut bypasses a correct item-linker refusal. Independently, product and tracer spellings still evade **both** refusal paths. The graph, stored-value and demo checks pass; they do not validate the identity selected from a refused product.

No tracked/application file was edited. All new audit files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m7/`. The specified whole-item fail-closed behavior and known M5-S2 are accepted. Whole-input stored-name precedence is evaluated separately from ordinary list parsing.

## Verification and evidence boundaries

- Required suite: **331 passed in 45.74 s**, exit 0, using `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`. Bytecode disabled; test temporary files confined to m7. [Output](root_checks/pytest.txt), [environment/hashes](root_checks/pytest_summary.json).
- Reused earlier scripts, rather than replacing their cases with new assertions: **13 inherited name scripts**, **six m6 supplements**, **all five original m1 name scripts**, **ten inherited CLI HTTP runners**, and **16 inherited DB/UI commands** completed with exit 0. [Name provenance and coverage](names/REPORT.md), [root copy manifest](root_checks/reuse_manifest.json), [m1–m5 HTTP execution](root_checks/execution_http.json), [m6 HTTP execution](root_checks/execution_m6_http.json), [DB/UI execution](db_ui/execution.json).
- Name coverage includes the earlier **113-case boundary matrix / 7,511 unique alias-key sweep**, the exact m6 **106-input / 8-QA** and **11-input reversed-qualifier** probes, and the frozen **1,648-question** no-relink comparison. The old no-relink patch became inert after the matcher moved to `_link`; a separately recorded mechanical adaptation patches the correct method. It preserves the exact historical inputs and yields **64 identical / 1,560 metadata-only / 24 semantic differences / 0 errors**. These are counterfactual comparisons, not 24 new regressions. [Adaptation and corpus verification](names/REPORT.md#reproduction-completeness).
- **6,469 live centres**, **22 relation/direction/type/role combinations**, **0 stored-row oracle mismatches**; **19/19 DB assertions** and **24/24 UI assertions** pass. Retirement/malformed/numeric mutations use isolated in-memory fixtures. [DB/UI report](db_ui/REPORT.md), [oracle](db_ui/db_validate.json), [DB assertions](db_ui/db_assertions.json), [UI assertions](db_ui/ui_assertions.json).
- **36/36 complete page-demo responses equal m6**; all **36 demo callbacks plus six map callbacks** pass. An additional original-CLI server receives all 36 demo payloads: **36 HTTP 200 / 36 exact response matches**. [Comparison](db_ui/ui_demo_compare.json), [HTTP](db_ui/demo_http.json), [callbacks](db_ui/ui_m3_checks.json).
- Fresh attacks: **93 exact inputs**, **10 QA cases**, **44 actual HTTP requests**, with guard-stage and predecessor comparisons. [Attack report](attacks/REPORT.md), [stage results](attacks/fresh_results.json), [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json). The original CLI served worktree-identical HTML. Owned fixed ports were **8901–8905**, used sequentially where shared; no use of 8765.
- UI scripts ran in Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs. This verifies generated markup, callback behavior and response ordering; it does **not** verify browser paint/layout or browser-executed exploit behavior. No browser-executed XSS certification is claimed.

## Blocking findings

### M7-B1 — P1: whole-name recognition overrides a refusal already made correctly by `_link`

Exact request:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb?"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns **HTTP 200**, **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, `matched_text:"Bevacizumab awwb"`, and `also:[]`. There is no separate accepted originator mention.

| Stage/control | Current result |
|---|---|
| `/api/resolve` | `ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583`, no refusal |
| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` |
| `_link` alone | only `Refused`, label `bevacizumab-awwb` |
| `_whole_name`, then public `link_entities` | Drug / CHEMBL1201583 |
| Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` | refused |
| Exact `b7a00f9` linker on `bevacizumab-awwb?` | refused |

**New versus the reviewed m6 HEAD.** `bevacizumab-awwb!` also regresses. Literal quoted `"bevacizumab-awwb"` demonstrates the same current shortcut bypass, although that quoted spelling also leaked in the predecessor. [Actual HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [current/previous stages](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent historical replay](names/fresh_results.json).

**Cause:** `src/query.py:138-142` returns `_whole_name` before consulting `_link`. The shortcut creates its key from punctuation-stripped text (`191-194`) but runs product/tracer guards on the original text (`195-196`). The anchored suffix pattern (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213`) misses the question mark; `canonical_key` (`339-346`) still obtains the originator alias. `_link` would remove that punctuation and refuse (`src/query.py:217-220,252-264`). `Engine.explore` trusts the shortcut's hit (`src/app.py:88-95`).

The exact prefixed QA control `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb?` returns **zero evidence**: its prefix prevents the whole-input shortcut and reaches the working item linker. This is a map-centre regression, not a claim that that precise QA sentence leaks.

**Required outcome:** a whole-name exception must preserve refusal on the representation that enables its positive match. It must not override the correct refusal merely because punctuation defeats a raw anchored predicate.

### M7-B2 — P1: product wrappers and Unicode hyphens defeat both guards

These satisfy the requested **both-paths-miss** attack:

| Exact input | Resolver | `_link` and actual map |
|---|---|---|
| `(bevacizumab-awwb)` | ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 | BEVACIZUMAB, 122 trials; no refusal |
| `[bevacizumab-awwb]` | same | same |
| `bevacizumab‑awwb` — internal U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN | same | same |
| `bio‑similar bevacizumab` — U+2011 | CONTAINED_ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 plus unresolved qualifier | same |

Bare ASCII `bevacizumab-awwb` and `bio-similar bevacizumab` refuse. Every row above has actual HTTP explore/resolve confirmation and direct `_link` evidence; no exception for a separately accepted molecule applies. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [stages and stored aliases](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent replay](names/m7_focus.json).

`Which trials evaluate (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00955305|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201583|NCT00955305`. `Which companies sponsor (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12**, first `ORG:roche|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL1201583|`. The U+2011 suffix and `bio‑similar` trial questions also return 12 originator rows over HTTP.

**Cause:** `_link` retains brackets (`src/query.py:217-220`), while the biosimilar suffix predicate anchors the whole input and accepts only ASCII hyphen/en dash (`src/textrules.py:156-157`). The `bio-similar` pattern permits only ASCII hyphen (`139-140`). The later canonical key removes/normalizes these distinctions (`339-346`). The resolver calls the same product predicate (`src/normalize.py:729-735`), so running it first provides no independent protection. `_qualified` simply retries these predicates (`src/query.py:99-110`).

These are **inherited**, confirmed against the exact prior linker. The parenthesized/bracketed probes already appeared in m6's supplementary attack and remain **NOT FIXED**, despite repair of m6's named U+3002 example. The repository's own refused ASCII controls establish the product premise; no external pharmacology assumption is needed.

**Required outcome:** equivalent wrappers/hyphen spellings of the refused product must not unlock its originator alias, in either map or QA.

### M7-B3 — P1: a space-separated isotope prefix passes both guards

**`89Zr trastuzumab`** returns HTTP 200, **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, `also:[]`. The resolver returns **ALIAS** using stored synonym **`89zr-trastuzumab`**; `_link` returns the same originator and `_resolver_blocked` is empty. Both `89Zr-trastuzumab` and `[89Zr] trastuzumab` refuse over HTTP. [HTTP/stages](attacks/REPORT.md#blocking-finding-c-a-space-separated-isotope-prefix-passes-both-gates), [stored alias](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

`Which trials evaluate 89Zr trastuzumab?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00016367|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201585|NCT00016367`, with stored intervention `Trastuzumab`. The bracketed QA control returns zero.

**Cause:** `src/textrules.py:303-305` requires a hyphen after an unbracketed isotope, or brackets around it. `is_isotope_label` recognizes the separate `89Zr` token (`289-298`), but the resolver tests the entire component (`src/normalize.py:740-741`), and `_qualified` never calls that token predicate (`src/query.py:105-110`). Canonical alias lookup then accepts the stored radiolabel synonym. This is **inherited**, not introduced by HEAD.

**Required outcome:** the isotope-bearing name must remain refused when the isotope and moiety are separated by whitespace; neither map nor QA should substitute the unlabelled originator.

### M7-S1 — P2: canonical equality is broader than exactly one stored name

| Exact input | Current map | `_link` / exact prior linker |
|---|---|---|
| `Erlotinib, hydrochloride` | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742, 31 trials | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553, 118 trials |
| `osimertinib,mesylate` | OSIMERTINIB MESYLATE / CHEMBL3545063, 7 trials | OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410, 79 trials |
| `is a` | ISATUXIMAB / CHEMBL3545131, 1 trial | no entity / not_found |

All three are actual HTTP reproductions and **new versus b7a00f9**. None exactly matches a stored alias, even case-insensitively; they only match the aggressive key. The comma inputs violate the declared list boundary. The ordinary-word input bypasses the explicit `is a` safeguard in `_link`. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [dictionary proof](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

`_whole_name` (`src/query.py:189-195`) erases punctuation and whitespace via `canonical_key` and never checks the stored name's actual token/punctuation boundaries; `_link` correctly enforces boundaries and the single-token abbreviation safeguard (`293-307`). Genuine complete-name controls `CP-358,774` and `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` have stored full-name matches and correctly retain their intended identities. Their exception need not accept arbitrary new comma lists. Prefixed salt QA stays on the parent drug; the salt counts themselves are accurately stored values.

### M7-S2 — P2: exact stored names are rejected before whole-name lookup

**`(E/Z)-AFATINIB`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1173655**, and `/api/resolve` returns **EXACT**. **`RO 09-1978/000`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1773**, with resolver **ALIAS**. Both `/api/explore` requests return HTTP 200 **not_found**, while the exact IDs draw live nodes. [Actual HTTP, stored-index proof and ID controls](names/alias_http.json).

`src/query.py:189-190` rejects slash/pipe or a listing word before checking whether the input is exactly one stored non-refused name. Thus step (2)'s intended precedence does not cover these valid lone names. The sweep has 20 formerly recognized names with no current Drug, including known M5-S2; it has **zero remaining wrong-ID cases among the 7,511 unique stored keys**. Some lost-alias resolver statuses differ, so the two positively resolved controls above are the strongest should-fix proof. [Complete list](names/names_m5_summary.json). No demo is lost.

### M7-S3 — P2: per-piece full resolution causes new multi-second lock contention

The old cubic all-substring defect remains repaired. The new resolver call for **every list piece**, including identical repeated pieces (`src/query.py:159-164`), introduces a different cost. A same-data, three-sample comparison loads the exact **b7a00f9 query module** and current query module without changing shared rules/data. A second measurement ran after all other audit workers stopped; this isolates audit workloads, not unrelated host activity.

| Exact linker input | Characters | b7a00f9 median | Current median |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `" ".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0219 s | 0.0298 s |
| `";".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0057 s | 0.2537 s |
| `";".join(["x"]*1000)` | 1,999 | 0.0512 s | 1.3120 s |
| `";".join(["x"]*2000)` | 3,999 | 0.1779 s | 2.5561 s |

The 2,000-piece stage check spends **2.5653 s** in `_resolver_blocked`, versus **0.2734 s** in `_link`. Measurements are separate calls and need not sum to a median. [Samples/stage timings](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_comparison.json), [reproducer](root_checks/fresh_cost.py).

Actual unchanged CLI HTTP, within existing caps:

- 499-character semicolon text: `/api/explore` **0.3160 s**, `/api/ask` **0.3148 s**, both 200.
- `POST /api/check` with `{"text":"EGFR","answer":";".join(["x"]*2000)}`: **7.7280 s**, HTTP 200.
- Ordinary EGFR map dispatched **0.1010 s** after that check: **7.6665 s**, HTTP 200. EGFR before/after: **0.0384 / 0.0412 s**. GET `/` remains responsive.
- First independent run also reproduced the delay: check **6.0783 s**, queued EGFR **6.0118 s**. No timeout/deadlock inference is needed; these responses completed.

[Isolated HTTP](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_http.json), [first HTTP run](root_checks/fresh_cost_http.json), [owned-server cleanup](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_lifecycle.json). The inherited m6 stress script independently records split_2000 at **3.4737 s** median linking / **7.0111 s** `Engine.check`; plain 2,000-token input is far cheaper. [Exact inherited stress](root_checks/cost_stress.json).

The application holds `engine.lock` across linking/checking (`src/app.py:247-263`), and the answer cap is 4,000 characters (`260`). Avoid repeating full resolver work for identical pieces and bound total work across every caller, while preserving the refusal semantics. This is a should-fix cost regression, separate from the originator-leak approval blockers. These timings are local observations, not universal latency guarantees or a claim that the entire algorithm has one asymptotic complexity.

# Independent relation-map review — round 6

Reviewed HEAD **`b7a00f9`**, including repairs `595f5f9` and `c43f911`; baseline feature commit `bfcb83d`. Date: 2026-11-03 KST. **Three reproducible originator-leak mechanisms block approval.** All exact named m1–m4 blockers remain fixed. M5's reported spaced-separator examples are fixed, but its earlier internal-separator attack still fails. M5-B2 and M5-S1 are fixed for their reported inputs; M5-S3's reproduced cost defect is fixed. ## Blocking findings

### M6-B1 — P1: a separator glued on both sides still hides a refused suffix

Exact request:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, and **no refused span**. The identical `/api/resolve` input returns `bevacizumab - awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted first drug, and `osimertinib: EXACT / CHEMBL3353410`. Thus the refused-product premise is demonstrated using the repository's own resolver. [Actual HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json), [independent replay](db_ui/interior_separator_probe.json).

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Actual result |
|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib?` | 12 originator rows; first `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib?` | 12 originator rows; first `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-154` splits comma/semicolon and whitespace, strips `/` and `|` only from the edges of a token, and creates boundaries only for `startswith`/`endswith`. Interior `awwb/osimertinib` remains one token. `_qualified` (`99-110`) never checks a run ending at `awwb`, so the anchored suffix guard (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213-223`) misses it. The separate bare `bevacizumab` token is then accepted at `src/query.py:212-237`, and `Engine.explore` trusts it at `src/app.py:88-95`.

`bevacizumab - awwb|osimertinib` also leaks. The pipe resolver itself misses that split, so the slash case is the strongest exact resolver comparison. Controls `bevacizumab - awwb/ osimertinib` and `bevacizumab - awwb / osimertinib` correctly select only OSIMERTINIB and disclose the refused product; bare `bevacizumab - awwb` refuses. These controls isolate the missing **interior** boundary.

Related consequences of the same mechanism are `Erlotinib/hydrochloride` and `Erlotinib|hydrochloride` assembling **CHEMBL1079742**, a salt across a declared item boundary; `biosimilar anastrozole/Arimidex` losing the separate Arimidex; and `osimertinib/EGFR` returning not_found. [HTTP](root_checks/alias_http.json). They are not counted as separate blockers.

**Status:** inherited under the exact `514929c` linker comparison; the slash input was already in m5's reused attack. M5-B1 is therefore **FIXED for the reported spaced examples, NOT FIXED as a mechanism**. [Previous/current comparison](attacks/fresh_results.json), [m5 replay](names/names_m5_attack.json).

**Required outcome:** all declared glued/free separators must form real item boundaries before guards and matching. Never select CHEMBL1201583 from this refused component; preserving the separately named osimertinib or refusing an ambiguous input is safe.

### M6-B2 — P1: edge punctuation is removed for alias matching after defeating refusal

Exact input **`bevacizumab-awwb。`**, with final **U+3002 IDEOGRAPHIC FULL STOP**, returns HTTP 200 and **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, matched alias `Bevacizumab awwb`, and no refusal. The same `/api/resolve` input yields a single `bevacizumab-awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` component with no accepted drug. [HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json).

`Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb。?` and `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab-awwb。?` each return **12 originator evidence rows**, with the same first trial/sponsor edges as B1. ASCII-period `bevacizumab-awwb.` and bare `bevacizumab-awwb` are refused; their trial/sponsor questions have **0 evidence**. Trailing U+2014, trailing ASCII hyphen and leading ASCII hyphen also produce the resolver/linker disagreement over HTTP.

**Cause:** the strip set at `src/query.py:147` differs from the resolver's edge-punctuation normalization (`src/textrules.py:283,382-383`). The suffix regex is anchored at both ends (`156-157`), so the retained punctuation defeats it. The later `canonical_key` call (`src/query.py:221`; `src/textrules.py:339-346`) removes that punctuation and recovers an alias for the originator. Whole-item and eight-word checks preserve the same offending character, so increasing their coverage cannot fix this mismatch.

**Status:** inherited under `514929c`, still a blocker. Quoted/bracketed suffix variants and some Unicode hyphens also fail both resolver and linker; those are explicitly distinguished from the exact U+3002 resolver disagreement in [independent findings](attacks/FINDINGS.md). No external pharmacology assumption is needed for the primary reproduction.

**Required outcome:** normalization that enables a positive alias must not remove evidence that prevented its refusal guard from matching. The U+3002 and hyphen-edge controls must refuse the product before mapping or retrieving originator evidence.

### M6-B3 — P1: forward-only qualifier carry loses a trailing qualifier inside one product description

Two exact parenthetical descriptions independently fail:

| Exact input | `/api/resolve` | `/api/explore` |
|---|---|---|
| `doxorubicin (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)` | One `doxorubicin` component, `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted drug; rules `extract_parenthetical`, `distinct_product_guard` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463, 3 trials, no refusal |
| `bevacizumab (investigational and biosimilar)` | One `bevacizumab` component, `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted drug; same rules | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials, no refusal |

These are qualified descriptions of the preceding drug, not two separately named drugs. [Actual HTTP](root_checks/carry_http.json), [independent current/predecessor comparison](attacks/reversed_results.json).

`Which trials evaluate doxorubicin (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)?` returns **3** originator rows, first `NCT01970540|EVALUATES|CHEMBL53463|NCT01970540`. Its sponsor question returns 3 rows, first `ORG:imunon|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL53463|`. The corresponding two bevacizumab questions return **12** originator rows each, beginning with B1's trial/sponsor edges.

**Cause:** `src/query.py:167-174` splits on `and` even inside the parenthetical qualifier. The drug is in the earlier, unqualified item. The later `liposomal formulation)` or `biosimilar)` item has no drug, so `192-197` stores it as `dangling` and continues. Carry is applied only to a **subsequent** drug (`183-184,199-201`); it never marks the earlier drug refused before final positive matching. The final qualifier can therefore disappear without even an output Refused entry. The full resolver preserves the original product context for its guard (`src/normalize.py:707,729-735`).

Controls `doxorubicin (liposomal formulation)` and the original M5-B2 `liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin` both refuse and produce **0 QA evidence**. Separate-drug controls `osimertinib and liposomal doxorubicin` and `doxorubicin and liposomal paclitaxel` retain the independently named first drug. [HTTP controls](root_checks/carry_http.json).

**Status:** inherited under `514929c`; the latest forward-carry repair does not solve this design failure. The stated carry direction explains the implementation but does not waive the approval bar against a refused product becoming its originator. This is an **under-refusal**, not the accepted same-item over-refusal or M5-S2.

**Required outcome:** preserve the qualifier's scope within an unambiguously single product description, or refuse that ambiguous product as a whole. Do not propagate every trailing qualifier backwards indiscriminately across genuinely separate named products.

## Should-fix and design consequences, separate from blockers

### M6-S1 — P2: complete stored names containing list punctuation can select a different drug

The strict item policy can do more than refuse a lone alias: six complete stored aliases in the reused 7,511-key sweep now select only another drug ID. Examples confirmed over actual HTTP:

| Complete stored name / alias | Stored unique identity | Current selected identity |
|---|---|---|
| `ENDOSTATIN, N-TERMINAL-MGGSHHHHH` — preferred name | CHEMBL4297946 | ENDOSTATIN / CHEMBL4297747 |
| `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` | EVEROLIMUS / CHEMBL1908360 | SIROLIMUS / CHEMBL413 |
| `CP-358,774` | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553 | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742 |

[HTTP and QA](root_checks/alias_http.json), [all six aliases, stored provenance and previous/current hits](names/names_m5_attack.json), [summary table](names/REPORT.md). The other examples are `Usp mab 002, monoclonal igg1`, `Usp mab 003, monoclonal igg1`, and `12,13-desoxyepothilone b, cis`. The endostatin exact-ID control selects the intended CHEMBL4297946. The complete endostatin name's trial QA instead retrieves two CHEMBL4297747 rows.

This follows the unconditional item boundaries at `src/query.py:143,216-217,277`. Because the requested design explicitly imposes those boundaries, this is reported as an identity tradeoff/should-fix, not used as an additional approval blocker or mislabeled as an over-refusal. It needs an explicit user-visible limitation or a safe disambiguation policy. Do not bypass product guards merely because a full alias exists.

The same sweep records **57** formerly recognized lone aliases with no current Drug hit against the exact bf436c6 comparison: the accepted pegylated alias plus **56** punctuation/list-word cases such as `ICI 182,780`, `CP-675,206`, `Nk cells, autologous`, and `Anusol plus hc`. These deliberate boundary losses are recorded without treating intended over-refusal as a new blocker. None appears in the page demos. [summary](names/names_m5_summary.json).

Previously recorded lowercase-ID/name handling, blank submit, raw 400 details and Infinity behavior remain as itemized above. No new misleading cut notice, graph relation/direction, stored field value, or generated-markup injection defect was established in the exercised paths.

## Cost, demo and boundary controls

The exact m5 performance script was reused; its `m4` baseline means bf436c6, not the slower m5 revision. Fresh instrumented HEAD uses **2,223 calls to each refusal predicate / 32,882 aggregate characters** for the 250-token plain case; the prior m5 report recorded 31,625 calls each / 10,479,750 characters. The bounded eight-word scan removes the former all-substring growth. [instrumentation](root_checks/perf_results.json).

| Exact fresh HTTP workload | Observed result |
|---|---|
| `/api/explore`, `" ".join(["x"] * 250)` — 499 chars | 200, **0.0302 s** |
| `/api/ask`, same 499 chars | 200, **0.0308 s** |
| `/api/explore`, 244 x tokens plus osimertinib — 499 chars | 200, **0.0796 s** |
| Four simultaneous 499-character explores | All 200, **0.1290 s** total wall time |
| Ordinary EGFR sent 0.1 s after one long lookup | 200, **0.0385 s**; not proof it waited on an occupied lock |
| 501-character explore | 400, **0.0030 s** |
| `/api/check`, question EGFR, 999-character x answer | 200, **0.1812 s**; following EGFR 200, **0.0402 s** |
| `/api/check`, question EGFR, 3,999-character x answer | 200, **0.3930 s** |

[HTTP timing/lock replay](root_checks/perf_http.json), [previous answer-timeout reproduction](root_checks/perf_check_http.json), [larger answer-field HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json). These are local observations, not universal latency guarantees; first replay workloads overlapped with other read-only audit work.

An additional isolated three-sample direct-linker check gives medians **0.0143, 0.0277, 0.0569, 0.1139 s** at 250/500/1,000/2,000 plain tokens. A 3,999-character semicolon-separated answer takes **0.1395 s** to link / **0.3340 s** for `Engine.check`; many matched drugs, many refused items and many joiners also complete below those check timings. [Exact inputs and measurements](root_checks/cost_stress.json), [reproducer](cost_stress.py). The overall linker still has per-segment rescans and growing set operations (`src/query.py:180-186,214`); this report does not infer global linear complexity from `_qualified` alone. No further practical cost blocker was demonstrated within the HTTP caps.

All **16** new padded suffix/tracer/liposomal/generic-version controls with 0/4/8/16 filler words refuse. No isolated eight-word-window regression was found. Forward qualifier-carry and standalone product controls (`Mvasi and bevacizumab`, bracketed/trademark Mvasi, `Abraxane and paclitaxel`) behave as specified. [fresh attack controls](attacks/fresh_results.json).

All seven actual HTTP QA demos retain evidence counts **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0**. Six full responses equal bfcb83d; Mvasi differs only in refusal metadata. All 36 complete demo responses equal m5. [HTTP demo comparison](root_checks/http_demo_results.json), [full demo comparison](db_ui/ui_demo_compare.json).

Ordinary Disease HTTP returns 2,774 Trials and 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 80.714 ms**. The original **24 requests / 12 workers** all return expected HTTP 200 shapes in **753.288 ms**. A 1,001-Drug fixture verifies all three count chunks. [HTTP](root_checks/http_results.json), [fixtures](db_ui/db_fixtures.json).

No application code was changed, so a KOA Studio / KhOpenApi original-versus-modified code comparison is not applicable. [Separate standards review](db_ui/standards_findings.md).

**Specification axis: three blocking refusal mechanisms**, plus the separate stored-name identity tradeoff and unchanged qualifications. All graph-edge, direction and value checks remain successful for the selected centres; that does not excuse selecting a refused product's originator. The page/docstring refusal promise still fails (`src/app.py:74-76`, `web/try.html:215`). README launch wording remains accurate (`README.md:566`, `README.ko.md:387`).

Snapshot: 7 retired nodes, 142 retired edges, no live edge with a retired endpoint, no self-loop, and all six node types covered. Malformed/retired/numeric cases use isolated in-memory fixtures. No model was called by the exercised application paths; `Engine.ask` sets `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). Application SQLite is not globally opened read-only, so this is executed-path and hash evidence, not a database-permission claim.

Final tracked/staged diff, every tracked file's SHA-256 comparison, database hash, owned-port closure and report-link checks are recorded in [integrity_final.json](integrity_final.json). Expected unchanged database SHA-256: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`.

## Round 4 (audit_evidence_claims/m4) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Shortest-span binding kept failing (wrong drug on ties, a six-token reach, qualifiers already consumed by the token pass).
Replaced by a fail-closed rule: text is cut into list items at , ; ， ； 、 and listing words; an item in which any run of
words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

## Round 5 (audit_evidence_claims/m5) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M5-B1 | a glued "/" or "|" ("bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib") hid the suffix guard; the alias key dropped the slash and linked BEVACIZUMAB | tokens are stripped of "/" and "|", which end the list item |
| M5-B2 | "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": "and" detached the bare qualifier | a qualifier item with no drug carries across a listing word to the next item's drugs; a product named by itself (in the index, or a known biosimilar/reformulation brand such as Mvasi, Abraxane) does not carry |
| M5-S1 | names were built across list items ("biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex" lost Arimidex; "Erlotinib, hydrochloride ..." became the salt) | drug spans and the positive matcher stay inside one item |
| M5-S3 | the all-runs scan was cubic (1.8 s at 250 words, under the lock) | whole item once for unanchored patterns, runs of at most eight words for anchored ones: 500 words in about 0.03 s |
| M5-S2 | "Pegylated interferon alfa-2a" (a stored alias of the pegylated drug itself) is refused | not changed: the resolver refuses it too, and exempting known aliases from the product guards would reopen M5-B1 (recorded as a known over-refusal) |

Tests 329.

## Round 6 (audit_evidence_claims/m6) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M6-B1 | an interior "/" or "|" ("bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib") hid the suffix | "/" and "|" split a word wherever they are and end the list item |
| M6-B2 | edge punctuation the guards kept but the alias key dropped ("bevacizumab-awwb。", trailing hyphen) | edge punctuation is stripped before the guards (brackets kept for isotopes) |
| M6-B3 | "and" inside a parenthetical description detached its qualifier from the preceding drug | listing words inside parentheses do not split |
| all | the linker kept re-implementing the resolver's refusals | the intervention resolver now runs on each list piece; a drug inside a piece it refuses as a distinct product or tracer is never linked (unless the same molecule is also accepted in another piece, which the list-item rule then governs) |
| M6-S1 | complete stored names with list punctuation picked another drug | an input that is exactly one stored name (and not refused) is read whole |

Tests 331.

## Round 7 (audit_evidence_claims/m7) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M7-B1 | the whole-name shortcut ran before the list linker and overrode its refusal ("bevacizumab-awwb?") | the shortcut runs last and only if neither the resolver nor the list linker refused anything |
| M7-B2 | wrappers and Unicode hyphens ("(bevacizumab-awwb)", U+2011) defeated both guards | input is NFKC-normalised and every dash folded to "-" before any check; all edge punctuation and wrappers are stripped from words |
| M7-B3 | "89Zr trastuzumab" (space-separated isotope) passed both guards | an isotope label anywhere in a list item qualifies the item |
| M7-S1 | the whole-name shortcut used the aggressive key ("is a", "Erlotinib, hydrochloride") | only a letter-for-letter stored name (after the same normalisation) is read whole |
| M7-S2 | exact stored names with "/" or a listing word were rejected first | the exact-name check no longer pre-rejects them |
| M7-S3 | the resolver ran on every piece | each distinct piece once, only if it holds a drug name, with a per-engine cache |

FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
13:   the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
14:   against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
15:   graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
16:   directly.
17: * **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
18:   reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
19: """

38: 
39: 
40: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
41: # evidence
42: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
43: @dataclass
44: class Evidence:
45:     statement: str
46:     edge_id: str
47:     relation: str
48:     source: str
49:     source_record: str | None
50:     fetched_at: str | None
51:     confidence: float
52:     tier: str | None = None
53:     url: str | None = None
54:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
55: 
56:     def citation(self) -> str:
57:         base = self.source_record or self.source
58:         return f"[{base}]"
59: 
60: 
61: @dataclass
62: class Answer:
63:     question: str
64:     intent: str
65:     entities: list[dict]
66:     text: str
67:     evidence: list[Evidence]
68:     generated_by: str
69:     checks: dict
70: 
71:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
72:         return {
73:             "question": self.question,
74:             "intent": self.intent,
75:             "entities": self.entities,
76:             "answer": self.text,
77:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
78:             "checks": self.checks,
79:             "evidence": [
80:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
81:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
82:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
83:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
84:                 for e in self.evidence
85:             ],
86:         }
87: 
88: 
89: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
90: # engine
91: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
92: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
93: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
94: # nanoparticles").
95: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
96:             "or/and", "and/or"}
97: 
98: 
99: 
100: # every Unicode dash and minus, read as a hyphen
101: _DASHES = r"[\u2010-\u2015\u2212\ufe58\ufe63\uff0d]"
102: 
103: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
104:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
105: 
106:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
107:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
108:     work grows linearly with the item."""
109:     text = " ".join(words)
110:     if T.is_distinct_product(text) or T.is_radiotracer(text):
111:         return True
112:     if any(T.is_isotope_label(w) for w in words):   # "89Zr trastuzumab", "[89Zr] ..."
113:         return True
114:     return any(T.is_distinct_product(w) or T.is_radiotracer(w)
115:                for w in (" ".join(words[i:j]) for i in range(len(words))
116:                          for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1)))
117: 
118: class OncoKG:
119:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
120:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
121:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
122:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
123:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
124:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
125:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
126:         self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
127:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
128:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
129:                         if t.get("symbol")}
130:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
131:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
132:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
133:         for row in self.conn.execute(
134:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
135:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
136:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
137:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
138:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
139:             if same in self.companies:
140:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
141:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
142: 
143:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
144:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
145:         # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
146:         question = re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", question or ""))
147:         blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
148:         found = self._link(question)
149:         whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
150:         if whole:
151:             return [whole]
152:         if not blocked:
153:             return found
154:         # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
155:         # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
156:         out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
157:         for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
158:             if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
159:                        for e in out):
160:                 out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
161:                             "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
162:                                    "originator molecule"})
163:         return out
164: 
165:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
166:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
167:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
168:         comps = []
169:         pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
170:         for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
171:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
172:             if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
173:                 continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
174:             if piece not in self._resolve_cache:
175:                 try:
176:                     self._resolve_cache[piece] = N.resolve_drug_string(
177:                         piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
178:                 except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
179:                     self._resolve_cache[piece] = []
180:                 if len(self._resolve_cache) > 4096:
181:                     self._resolve_cache.clear()
182:             comps += self._resolve_cache[piece]
183:         out: dict = {}
184:         accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
185:                     if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
186:         for c in comps:
187:             piece = c.get("component") or ""
188:             if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
189:                     or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and T.is_radiotracer(piece))):
190:                 continue
191:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
192:             words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
193:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
194:                 key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
195:                 for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
196:                     # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
197:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
198:                     if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
199:                         out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
200:         return out
201: 
202:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
203:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
204:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
205: 
206:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
207:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
208:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
209:             return None
210:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
211:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name) or _qualified(name.split()):
212:             return None
213:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
214:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
215:                  if re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", h["alias"])).lower()
216:                  == name.lower()]
217:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
218:             return None
219:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
220:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
221:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
222: 
223:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
224:         found: list[dict] = []
225:         seen: set = set()
226:         tokens: list[str] = []
227:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
228:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
229:         ci, depth = 0, 0
230:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
231:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
232:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
233:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
234:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
235:                     if j:
236:                         ci += 1
237:                     if not part:
238:                         continue
239:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
240:                         # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
241:                         # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
242:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
243:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
244:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
245:                     else:
246:                         t = ""
247:                     if t:
248:                         tokens.append(t)
249:                         clause.append(ci)
250:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
251:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
252:             ci += 1
253: 
254:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
255:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
256:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
257:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
258:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
259:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
260:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
261:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
262:         seg: list[int] = []
263:         n = 0
264:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
265:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
266:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
267:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
268:                 n += 1
269:                 seg.append(-1)
270:                 continue
271:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
272:                 n += 1
273:             seg.append(n)
274:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
275:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
276:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
277:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
278:         prev_end = -1
279:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
280:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
281:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
282:             joined = dangling is not None and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0])) \
283:                 and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
284:             prev_end = idx[-1]
285:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
286:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
287:                 named = any(k in refused and (
288:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
289:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
290:                     for k in idx)
291:                 if not hit and not named:
292:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
293:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
294:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
295:                     dangling = text
296:                     continue
297:                 dangling = None
298:             elif joined and hit:
299:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
300:                 dangling = None
301:             else:
302:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
303:                 continue
304:             refused.update(hit)
305:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
306:                           "matched_text": text,
307:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
308:                                  "the originator molecule"})
309: 
310:         matched: set[int] = set()
311:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
312:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
313:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
314:                     continue
315:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
316:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
317:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
318:                 if len(window) < 3:
319:                     continue
320:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
321: 
322:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
323:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
324:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
325:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
326:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
327:                     hits = None
328:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
329:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
330:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
331:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
332:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
333:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
334:                                       "matched_text": window,
335:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
336:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
337:                     continue
338: 
339:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:
340:                     t = self.targets[window.lower()]

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_verification.py
"""Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
TMP = OUT / "tmp"
TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
       "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}

def git(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
         "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
(OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
    "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
start = time.perf_counter()
run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
(OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
           "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
           "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
           "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
(OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
raise SystemExit(run.returncode)
